▶ position manager: structural and broker protection
  ✔ structural SL: a CONFIRMED close beyond the stop exits at that close (thesis invalidation), before the broker SL (1.1788ms)
  ✔ broker SL: an intrabar spike through 1.5 x structural + spread closes at exactly that distance plus slippage and is never widened (0.3246ms)
  ✔ thesis deterioration (3 adverse closes, beyond half R and the entry bar) exits early with a smaller loss than invalidation (0.3293ms)
  ✔ SELL mirror: spread is paid on the exit side and the structural stop is above the fill (0.2434ms)
✔ position manager: structural and broker protection (2.3467ms)
▶ position manager: HOLD / PROFIT_AVAILABLE / PROTECT / HARVEST / EXIT
  ✔ profit milestone detection: PROFIT_AVAILABLE at the first close with MFE >= trigger R (0.75 R for D1, 1.0 R for D2) (0.4283ms)
  ✔ HOLD: with no profit available and no deterioration the manager holds and leaves protection unchanged (0.1799ms)
  ✔ HARVEST on weak continuation: a spike to the trigger followed by a weak close banks at that close (0.1739ms)
  ✔ PROTECT + RUN on strong continuation: floor activates from the next bar, ratchets upward, and the floor touch banks the floor (0.2052ms)
  ✔ EXIT on adverse break during a protected run (harvest at the close, never below the floor) (0.1826ms)
  ✔ HARVEST when the remaining reward compresses (distance to TP2 < 0.5 ATR) (0.1018ms)
  ✔ simple bank (CH-B) closes at the first close after 1 R (0.0754ms)
  ✔ production-like (CH-A) closes on retrace >= 0.5 of the best excursion after 1 R, or at the +30 USD monetary target (0.2712ms)
✔ position manager: HOLD / PROFIT_AVAILABLE / PROTECT / HARVEST / EXIT (1.8631ms)

▶ cdpLock: tryAcquireCdpLock() basic acquire/release

  ✔ releaseCdpLock only removes a lock this SAME process holds (4.8412ms)
  ✔ releaseCdpLock never removes another process's lock, even a stale one it did not itself reclaim (3.6047ms)
  ✔ releaseCdpLock never throws even if the lock file is already gone (0.8946ms)
✔ cdpLock: tryAcquireCdpLock() basic acquire/release (19.1016ms)
▶ cdpLock: stale-lock reclaim

  ✔ reclaims a lock older than maxAgeMs even if the holder PID still reports alive ("no permanent stale lock") (4.6373ms)
  ✔ does NOT reclaim a fresh lock from a still-alive holder (3.0604ms)

✔ cdpLock: stale-lock reclaim (16.4309ms)

  ✔ tryAcquireCdpLock throws CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT if the CURRENT process already holds the lock (4.8691ms)
  ✔ withCdpLock rejects with CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT (not a hang) if fn() tries to re-acquire the SAME lock nested inside itself (4.6911ms)
✔ cdpLock: re-entrancy -- fails fast, never deadlocks (9.8634ms)
▶ cdpLock: acquireCdpLock() polling + timeout
  ✔ polls until a busy lock frees up, then acquires it (proves serialization: a second "operation" WAITS its turn rather than racing) (42.9074ms)
  ✔ throws CDP_LOCK_TIMEOUT rather than hanging forever if the lock never frees up (45.514ms)
✔ cdpLock: acquireCdpLock() polling + timeout (88.8808ms)
▶ cdpLock: withCdpLock() always releases
  ✔ releases after fn() resolves successfully (4.5398ms)
  ✔ releases after fn() throws (a WAIT-shaped/error result never leaves the lock held) (5.4818ms)
  ✔ two sequential withCdpLock() calls on the SAME path never see each other's lock (serialized, not deadlocked) (8.0481ms)
  ✔ proves cross-process mutual exclusion via a simulated different-pid holder: a competing acquisition never wins while the (other-process) holder is live and fresh, and wins immediately once it is not (15.1253ms)
  ✔ within the SAME process, a second (non-nested but concurrent) withCdpLock() call on the SAME path fails fast with CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT rather than hanging or double-entering fn() (27.7374ms)
  ✔ leaves no leftover .tmp- files behind after normal sequential use (Windows-race hardening cleans up its own losing temp files) (46.7222ms)
✔ cdpLock: withCdpLock() always releases (108.4009ms)

  ✔ overextension_state=ENTRY_LATE with an otherwise-complete BUY is a contradiction (0.3328ms)
  ✔ BUY and SELL cannot both appear in a single fixture (ACTION is a single field) — structurally impossible by the contract's own shape (0.4832ms)

  ✔ a candle fully ABOVE rangeLow (low never touches it) → NO trigger, even if close is arbitrarily close to rangeLow (0.8523ms)

▶ P4B runtime-safety: every array.size()-bounded for-loop is guarded against an empty array
  ✔ every "for i = 0 to array.size(IDENT) - 1" is immediately preceded (skipping blank/comment lines) by "if array.size(IDENT) > 0" (4.0121ms)
  ✔ the P5 OUTCOME RESOLUTION backward loop (for safe in-loop array.remove) is guarded against an empty openSignals array exactly like every forward loop (0.6165ms)
  ✔ array.remove() now legitimately exists (P5 review/fix), but ONLY as the one safe in-loop removal inside the guarded, backward-iterating P5 resolution loop — no other unsafe indexed-collection function (array.first/array.last/array.pop/array.insert) exists anywhere (1.0791ms)
  ✔ array.shift() call sites remain safe: P2's two capacity-eviction sites remain guarded by "size > maxActiveLevels" preceded by array.push() (guaranteeing size >= 1); P5's recentHistory (closed-only) eviction is guarded by "size >= p5MaxTrackedSignals" instead (a capacity check ahead of the push, not a post-push overflow check — still provably safe, since array.shift() on an empty array is never reachable there either: the guard only ever removes an EXISTING oldest element to make room, and array.push() unconditionally follows it in the same block). Critically, openSignals (the never-evicted OPEN tracker) must have NO array.shift() call anywhere. (2.618ms)
  ✔ the debug-table convenience reads (last-level consumed/swept) remain guarded by their own ternary size>0 check — unchanged by this patch (0.6281ms)
